# INTRODUCTION-B.O.B

## CIC Operations Sector Book of Basics

Introduction Purpose and Intended Audience

The purpose of the Operations Sector Book of Basics is to better inform CIC senior management while supporting departmental operational planning, analysis and enhance decision making. It contains core operational data that presents intake, output (including decisions, acceptance rates, documents issued and admissions processed), processing time and inventory for the department's key business lines which include: processing of Permanent Residents; Temporary Residents; Citizenship; Refugee Claimants; Permanent Resident Cards; and Medical Assessment. The Book of Basics is also intended to respond to the department's need for timely and accurate operational data and to increase consistency in the use and presentation of Immigration and Citizenship data. This report, to be prepared on a quarterly basis by calendar year, will complement existing reports produced by other Sectors and branches of the Department, and will provide critical analytical pieces such as briefing (BN) and Question Period (QP) notes. Additionally, the Operational Dashboard will continue as the Sector's primary reporting and monitoring tool on key high- level commitments. The various data included in the report represents work done by the Citizenship and Immigration Canada (CIC) Operations Sector which includes the following areas of the department: Overseas Regions; In-Canada Regions; Case Processing Region; as well as some activities conducted by the Immigration and Refugee Board (IRB). Please note: official permanent and temporary resident admission data is produced by Research and Evaluation (R&E) and is available in the Facts & Figures annual publication ([http://www.cic.gc.ca/english/resources/statistics/menu-](http://www.cic.gc.ca/english/resources/statistics/menu-) fact.asp).

#### The Report's Focus

The bulk of data presented in the Book of Basics refers to persons as opposed to cases (unless otherwise stated). The primary purpose for representing the data in this manner is to align with how key departmental objectives are expressed, for example, inventories and admission ranges are reported in persons. It is also important to note that the persons count would include both the principal applicants and associated dependants.

Most of the tables in the report provide data for the past five consecutive calendar years and then compare the most recent quarter (cumulative year to date) of this calendar year to the same period of the previous year. There are two exceptions to this: First, in the case of inventories, the most recent quarter is compared to the prior quarter; and Second, in the case of processing times, the YTD column provides a rolling 12 month period including the most recent quarter and compares this to the same period of the prior year. The percentage change column displays the percentage Increase/decrease between current and previous year-to-date quarters. In tables that show top 10 offices or countries. all of the data is sorted in descending order according to the most recent year-to-date data; consequently, the five prior years displayed may not, nor are intended to, be ranked in the proper descending order.

Each publication of the Book of Basics will reflect updated operational data for previous time periods. Please note that this may resutt in changes to figures reported in previous releases; for example, applications received data for a prior period may increase over time as they are entered into the system of record and back-dated according to original date of receipt. Due to the lag time in entering application data, this also results in underreported intake figures for the most recent YTD data. This has a significant impact on the accuracy of data reported in intake tables; in particular: 1) the percentage change between prior YTD and current YTD is ovérstated; and 2) the order of countries/offices listed in Top 10 tables is adversely affected.

Any level of detail not provided can be requested through standard ad-hoc requests to the Performance Management Unif's mailbox: nhq-ops-stats@cic.gc.ca

# INTRODUCTION-B.O.B

## CIC Operations Sector Book of Basics

#### 2014 Q 4 Update

##### Changes to the current release

Throughout 2014, CIC has undergone major technological changes in data warehousing and business intelligence reporting. Specifically, the conventional reporting platform has been upgraded from Cognos 7 to Cognos 10.2, which

#### brings significant reporting functionality enhancement. In addition, all major systems used by Operations are being

integrated in one Enterprise Data Warehousing, i.e., GCMS, FOSS, CAIPS, CPC and NCMS, which closes a number of reporting gaps in the past. These changes offer new ways of business intelligence reporting. As results, all existing reports must be re-developed to adapt to the new reporting infrastructure, including Book of Basics (BOB).

In addition, we took this opportunity to re-design some sections of Book of Basics in order to accommodate significant business changes introduced over the last year. New business lines are added, and office and regional breakdowns were modified to align with the new integrated network approach.

Although the majority of BOB Re-design is completed, about 10% of the content requires further development, which is to be finalized in January 2015. All the data in this release has been verified and can be used. Some figures that were in previous version of BoB and are still missing in this version will become available in the Q4 2014 release in January 2015.

#### New or modified business lines and business line breakdowns: (Economic)Skilled Trades, Federal vs. Quebec

#### Entrepreneur, Federal vs. Quebec Investor, Federal vs. Quebec Self Employed, (non-Economic) Spouses &

#### Partners, Children & Other Family Class, (H&C) Federal vs. Quebec Government-assisted Refugees, Visa Office-

Referred, H&C Straight, H&C with Discrimination.

Improvement in counting methodology: Due to system and process changes, there were some counting rules issues with the H&C and Public Policy categories in previous BOB releases. These issues have been addressed and the data presented is now an accurate reflection of business practice.

New operating network point of service breakdowns: Traditionally, the operating network was broken down by Overseas and In-Canada. Such breakdown is no longer valid, as the network has become integrated, i.e., most applications are partially processed in various offices. In BOB, the Post of Service is now broken down to Abroad, Centralized Processing Region, Inland, Backlog Reduction Office to reflect various sub-networks.

Top 10 Offices – Application Received: This table is removed, as applications received in a particular office may be processed elsewhere. The source of applications is now reflected in Point of Service – Applications Received and Top 10 Source Countries-Applications Received tables, while the workload is reflected in the Top 10 Offices-Authorization of confirmations of Permanente Residence and Point of Service Inventory tables.

#### Permanent Residents Planned Admission Level and Operational Target: Only the current calendar year (2014)

#### Operational Target and Achievement are available in this release. The historical and current calendar years

Admission Levels wit be avallable in Q1 2015 release.

#### Temporary Residents: Component specific to each category, such as processing times, education level for Study

Permit., will be provided in a1, 2015.

##### Upcoming improvement in future releases

to lack ofdata integration. The newly integrated data is curréntly in the testing phase to allow consistent reporting on sponsorship.

# INTRODUCTION-B.O.B CIC Operations Sector Book of Basics

Data Sources

The data used to populate this report are extracted from many different sources including both electronic systems and manual reporting. The main source of data for the Book of Basics is the CIC Data Warehouse, which is a collection of data extracted from: Computer-Assisted Immigration Processing System (CAIPS); Field Operations Support System (FOSS); and in some cases, the National Case Management System (NCMS). In addition, this report also relies on data from thé following sources: Card Control System (CCŠ); Case Processing Centre (CPC) Reports; Global Case Management System (GCMS); Immigration and Refugee Board (IRB) Reports; Pulse Reports; Report on Operations (ROO); and Workloads, Inventories and Priorities (WIP) Reports. Please note that tables sourced as “dwsweb;IRB/irbrpd” were produced by CIC with IRB data.

Challenges In preparing a comprehensive report for CIC, reliable and timely data must be extracted from a multitude of systems, covering a broad range of departmental processes. Data gaps still exist, which impact on the accuracy and timeliness of the data, as well as the ability to conduct proper analysis and present findings. Although our level of confidence with respect to the information generated has increased over time, there are still some areas where validity of the data needs to be better established.
